Title: A Confutation of Certaine Articles delivered unto the Familye of Love, with the exposition of Theophilus, a supposed Elder in the sayd Familye, upon the same Articles
Creator: William Wilkinson, of Cambridge; with the Familist articles and the exposition of "Theophilus", an Elder of the English Family of Love, printed for confutation, and prefatory matter by others
Publisher: London: John Daye, dwelling over Aldersgate ("Alder gate", "cum priuilegio Regiae Maiestatis" in the OCR), 1579 ("Anno. 1579" at about file line 548)
Publication Date: 1579
Rights: Public Domain (published 1579; public domain by date)
Source: Internet Archive item bim_early-english-books-1475-1640_confutation-of-certaine-_wilkinson-william_1579, file bim_early-english-books-1475-1640_confutation-of-certaine-_wilkinson-william_1579_djvu.txt, fetched directly by the library/source-research thread (Era VII session, one-world agent for family-of-love-and-spiritualists-franck-niclaes), 2026-10-16; host rights statement: none on the item (no possible-copyright-status and no licence field; collections pub_early-english-books-*, bim_microfilm; an EEBO microfilm scan contributed by Internet Archive, not access-restricted).
Provenance note: Header prepended; the OCR text follows unchanged apart from long-s to s normalisation (5414 occurrences). Rights basis restated: an Elizabethan London print, the year 1579 read in the OCR of the front matter and given by the host, is public domain by date; no modern editorial text is in the file.
Content note: The whole print: the dedication to Richard Cox, Bishop of Ely (file line 195), the prefatory epistles and the summary of H. N.'s doctrine "gathered out of HN. his Euangelie" (the "1. Article. HN. sayth..." list from about line 702), then the articles of the Family of Love one by one, each followed by "Theophilus his exposition" and Wilkinson's confutation (the first article "No Church" from about line 2999; "Theophilus his exposition" at line 3016). A hostile Elizabethan witness that also preserves the English Familists' own articles and an Elder's commentary on them. EEBO microfilm OCR of a black-letter print with much marginal apparatus, sampled at five points and found readable but uneven (about 0.87 of tokens read as words; the title page and some margins are noise); check quotations against the page images.
